Вместо дорогого итеративного денойзинга используется одношаговое извлечение представлений из шума, а для обучения применяется self-distillation, где целевыми значениями служат представления реальных будущих кадров. Модель дополнительно получает историю наблюдений как временной контекст, что делает предсказания точнее. На реальных задачах манипуляции FRED превосходит конкурентов, особенно на длинных и out-of-domain задачах, и значительно улучшает перенос навыков с человека на робота.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Точнее предсказываем будущее — умнее действуем роботы"}

Abstract SEAM initializes unseen token embeddings from subtokens via spectral filtering and ridge regression, reducing training data needs by 75% for low-resource text-to-speech vocabulary expansion.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Expanding the vocabulary of conditional text-to-speech (TTS) models typically requires extensive fine-tuning with paired audio, creating a major bottleneck for low-resource and cross-lingual adaptation. To address this, we introduce SEAM, a zero-audio initialization method that constructs embeddings for unseen tokens from known subtokens. Unlike standard pooling strategies that ignore sequential structure, SEAM applies a spectral filter to preserve temporal phonetic order and uses closed-form ridge regression to align the new embeddings with the pretrained conditioning manifold. Across three languages and two TTS architectures, SEAM approaches or matches baseline performance using up to four times less training data, reducing sample complexity by 75\% compared to random initialization and mean pooling. Our approach provides a lightweight, reproducible, and theoretically grounded solution for vocabulary expansion in low-resource speech synthesis.

Abstract SkeLAM decomposes latent actions into skeleton-guided kinematic and residual components to improve controllable robot world modeling and motion transfer.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Robot world models simulate how environments evolve in response to robot actions. Latent action models enable learning dynamics from videos by encoding visual transitions as proxy actions. However, reconstruction-driven latent actions mix robot motion with object responses and other scene changes, complicating their correspondence with robot controls. We propose SkeLAM, a skeleton-guided latent action model for controllable robot world modeling. SkeLAM decomposes latent actions into a kinematic component grounded by robot skeleton supervision and a residual component retaining complementary visual dynamics. Cross-modal swap reconstruction encourages RGB-derived and skeleton-derived kinematic representations to be interchangeable during video reconstruction. The world model incorporates both components through separate conditioning pathways, while branch dropout reduces its dependence on future-derived residual information. A lightweight adapter maps robot actions to the kinematic space, and subsequent world-model fine-tuning adapts generation to these predicted conditions. At inference, the model generates future observations from the current observation and robot actions. On DROID, SkeLAM reduces end-effector ADE by 19.5% and LPIPS by 11.1% compared with DreamDojo. Motion probing supports kinematic specialization, while qualitative results demonstrate zero-shot human-to-robot motion transfer in generated rollouts. Policy evaluation shows agreement between predicted success rates and those measured in real robot executions.
[23.10.2026 06:57] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет SkeLAM — модель латентных действий, направляемая скелетом робота, которая разделяет латентные действия на кинематическую компоненту (опирающуюся на скелетную supervision) и остаточную компоненту с дополнительной визуальной динамикой. Для повышения взаимозаменяемости представлений используется cross-modal swap reconstruction, а world model учитывает обе компоненты через отдельные пути conditioning и branch dropout. На бенчмарке DROID SkeLAM снижает ADE конечного эффектора на 19.5% и LPIPS на 11.1% по сравнению с DreamDojo, а также демонстрирует zero-shot перенос движений человека на робота. Abstract Foundation4D is a unified feed-forward model that jointly estimates geometry, detects objects, predicts semantic occupancy, and reconstructs Gaussians from multi-camera driving video, achieving state-of-the-art results across several benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We introduce Foundation4D, a unified feed-forward model for geometry estimation, 3D detection, semantic occupancy, and Gaussian reconstruction from multi-camera driving video. A shared backbone uses factorized spatiotemporal attention to efficiently combine observations across cameras and time. Our video inference pipeline uses pose graph optimization to refine camera trajectories and assembles object tracks across input windows. We also develop a data annotation pipeline and release PAI-Foundation4D, a benchmark with dense depth and semantic occupancy labels for 1,253 driving clips. With benchmark-specific fine-tuning, Foundation4D achieves the lowest depth AbsRel on Waymo and nuScenes, the highest detection mAP on nuScenes, and the highest occupancy mIoU on OpenOccupancy among the compared methods. Our task-combination study shows benefits for both detection and geometry: co-training detection with geometry and occupancy improves top-class mAP by 1.2 points over detection-only training, while co-training geometry with detection reduces camera-to-rig translation error by 13.4% relative to geometry-only training. We further use the predicted geometry and trajectories as priors for lidarless NuRec, supporting reconstruction of driving scenes for simulation.
[23.10.2026 06:57] Response: {"desc": "Foundation4D — это единая feed-forward модель, которая одновременно оценивает геометрию сцены, детектирует объекты, предсказывает семантическую occupancy и реконструирует Gaussians по видео с нескольких камер автомобиля. Общий backbone с факторизованным spatiotemporal attention эффективно объединяет наблюдения между камерами и во времени, а pose graph optimization уточняет траектории камеры и собирает треки объектов. Авторы также выпустили датасет PAI-Foundation4D с плотными разметками глубины и semantic occupancy для 1253 клипов. Совместное обучение детекции с геометрией и occupancy повышает mAP, а предсказанная геометрия служит приором для lidarless NuRec-реконструкции сцен для симуляции.", "emoji": "🚗", "title": "Одна модель для геометрии, объектов и 3D-сцены по видео с камер авто"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Foundation4D is a unified feed-forward model that jointly estimates geometry, detects objects, predicts semantic occupancy, and reconstructs Gaussians from multi-camera driving video, achieving state-of-the-art results across several benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We introduce Foundation4D, a unified feed-forward model for geometry estimation, 3D detection, semantic occupancy, and Gaussian reconstruction from multi-camera driving video. A shared backbone uses factorized spatiotemporal attention to efficiently combine observations across cameras and time. Our video inference pipeline uses pose graph optimization to refine camera trajectories and assembles object tracks across input windows. We also develop a data annotation pipeline and release PAI-Foundation4D, a benchmark with dense depth and semantic occupancy labels for 1,253 driving clips. With benchmark-specific fine-tuning, Foundation4D achieves the lowest depth AbsRel on Waymo and nuScenes, the highest detection mAP on nuScenes, and the highest occupancy mIoU on OpenOccupancy among the compared methods. Our task-combination study shows benefits for both detection and geometry: co-training detection with geometry and occupancy improves top-class mAP by 1.2 points over detection-only training, while co-training geometry with detection reduces camera-to-rig translation error by 13.4% relative to geometry-only training. We further use the predicted geometry and trajectories as priors for lidarless NuRec, supporting reconstruction of driving scenes for simulation."
